```
{"stdout": "\"\"\"System prompt for review_hypo — adversarial hypothesis review before invention loop.\n\nReview only (no revision). Uses web search to ground review in evidence.\nScoring scales and review principles come from the shared review_rubric\ncomponent; hypothesis-specific phrasing is passed as parameters.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.aii_context import get_aii_context\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.ambition import AMBITION_REVIEW_PRINCIPLES\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.review_rubric import get_review_rubric\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.tool_calling import get_web_tool_guidance\n\n_ASSESSMENT_DIMENSIONS = \"\"\"(a) Originality: Are the ideas new? Novel combination of known techniques? Clear\n    differentiation from prior work? Is related work adequately cited?\n(b) Quality: Is the proposal technically sound? Are claims well supported? Is the\n    methodology appropriate? Are the authors honest about limitations?\n(c) Clarity: Is the hypothesis clearly written and well organized? Does it provide\n    enough information for an expert to understand and evaluate it?\n(d) Significance: Are the expected results important? Would others build on this?\n    Does it address a meaningful problem better than prior work?\n(e) Fidelity to the user's request: Does this hypothesis answer the request the run\n    was commissioned on, shown verbatim in the prompt? Are the subjects, the\n    deliverable and the measurement the ones that were asked for, or has the\n    hypothesis moved onto a neighbouring question that happens to be freer?\"\"\"\n\n\ndef PROMPT_REVIEW(context: str) -> str:\n    rubric = get_review_rubric(\n        assessment_dimensions=_ASSESSMENT_DIMENSIONS,\n        soundness_desc=\"soundness of the technical claims and proposed methodology\",\n        contribution_desc=(\n            \"quality of the overall contribution, importance of questions asked,\\n\"\n            \"originality of ideas, value to the broader research community\"\n        ),\n        major_issue_meaning=\"would waste compute if not fixed\",\n        extra_principles=(\n            \"- Score the fidelity dimension on the verbatim request in the prompt. A\"\n            \" hypothesis that answers a DIFFERENT question than the user asked scores 1\"\n            \" there and earns a MAJOR critique, whatever its originality, soundness or\"\n            \" significance — a novel answer to a question nobody asked is a failed run\",\n            \"- Rank a fidelity critique FIRST, ahead of the score-impact ordering. Every\"\n            \" other critique improves an answer; this one decides whether it is an answer\"\n            \" to the right question. Say which subject, deliverable or measurement from the\"\n            \" request went missing, and what restores it\",\n            \"- Flag fatal flaws that would make experiments pointless if not addressed first\",\n            \"- Screen the hypothesis for prior art before any compute is spent. Search the\"\n            \" web for the proposed idea, its method name, and its central claim. If the\"\n            \" idea already exists, say so and name the source — this is the cheapest point\"\n            \" in the pipeline to catch it\",\n            \"- Distinguish a genuinely new idea from a restatement of known work in new\"\n            \" vocabulary. Coining a term for an existing method is not originality, and\"\n            \" should be scored as a major issue\",\n            *AMBITION_REVIEW_PRINCIPLES,\n        ),\n    )\n    return f\"\"\"{context}\n\nROLE: You are a very experienced and critical conference reviewer.\nYour expertise spans the domain of the hypothesis under review.\nYou have served on program committees at top-tier venues in the relevant field.\n\nTASK: Perform a deep and honest review (at the level of a top-tier venue submission) of\nthis research hypothesis BEFORE any experiments have been run.\n\nGOAL: Your review feeds directly back to the hypothesis author. The objective is to\nmaximize the overall review score in subsequent rounds. Every piece of feedback you\ngive should be written with this goal in mind — prioritize the critiques and suggestions\nthat would produce the largest score improvement if addressed. Don't waste the author's\niteration budget on low-impact polish when there are score-blocking issues to fix.\n\n{rubric}\n\n{get_web_tool_guidance()}\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get_review() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Get system prompt for hypothesis review.\"\"\"\n    return PROMPT_REVIEW(context=get_aii_context(focus=\"review_hypo\"))", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

```
{"stdout": "\"\"\"User prompts for review_hypo — hypothesis review before invention loop.\n\nReview only (no revision). Feedback goes back to gen_hypo at the loop level.\nMirrors the structure of review_paper (Step 3.5) but operates on hypotheses, not papers.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.domain_handbooks import get_domain_handbooks_section\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.hypothesis_display import hypothesis_for_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.review_rubric import REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.untrusted_framing import UNTRUSTED_CONTENT_FRAMING\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_folder import get_user_folder_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_request import get_user_request_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.utils import to_prompt_yaml\n\n#: Replaces the default \"your job is NOT to satisfy that request directly\" note\n#: (``components/user_request.py``) for this step: the reviewer does not act on\n#: the request, but it is the yardstick for the fidelity dimension.\nOBJECTIVE_NOTE = (\n    \"That request is what the hypothesis under review was commissioned to answer, and it \"\n    \"is the yardstick for the fidelity dimension of your review. Judge the hypothesis \"\n    \"against it; do not act on it yourself.\"\n)\n\n\ndef PROMPT_REVIEW(\n    hypothesis_text: str,\n    previous_feedback_text: str,\n    previous_hypothesis_text: str,\n    commissioned_request: str = \"\",\n) -> str:\n    # The fidelity dimension needs the request in the same message as the\n    # hypothesis, not a pointer at another one: a reviewer cannot judge whether\n    # a hypothesis answers a question it was only told exists.\n    request_block = (\n        f\"\"\"<commissioned_request>\nThe user's request this run exists to answer, verbatim. {UNTRUSTED_CONTENT_FRAMING}\n\n{commissioned_request.strip()}\n</commissioned_request>\n\n\"\"\"\n        if commissioned_request.strip()\n        else \"\"\n    )\n    # Name the block when it is there; a run started without a prompt (fork,\n    # resume, standalone call) still scores the dimension, off the separate\n    # request message, rather than being pointed at a block that never rendered.\n    request_ref = (\n        \"the <commissioned_request> above\" if request_block else \"the user's original request\"\n    )\n    return f\"\"\"{REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK}\n\n{request_block}<hypothesis>\n{hypothesis_text}\n</hypothesis>\n\n<review_context>\nNo experiments have been run yet — evaluate the hypothesis purely on its merits.\n</review_context>\n\n{\n        get_domain_handbooks_section(\n            focus=\"judging whether the hypothesis is genuinely novel versus already-done or a known dead end in this field\"\n        )\n    }\n\n{\n        f'''<previous_hypothesis>\nThe hypothesis from the PREVIOUS iteration (before the revision under review).\nUse this to classify how the current hypothesis relates to it (see the H↔H\nedge instructions in the task).\n\n{previous_hypothesis_text}\n</previous_hypothesis>'''\n        if previous_hypothesis_text\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n{\n        f'''<previous_review>\nCritiques from the previous review. Check which ones have been addressed\nin the revised hypothesis. Do NOT re-raise critiques that have been adequately fixed.\nOnly re-raise if the fix is insufficient.\n\n{previous_feedback_text}\n</previous_review>'''\n        if previous_feedback_text\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n<task>\nProvide a thorough peer review of this research hypothesis.\n\nSTEP 1 — GROUND YOUR REVIEW IN EVIDENCE:\nBefore writing critiques, search for relevant context to make your review authoritative:\n- Search for accepted papers at top venues in this area — what level of\n  contribution gets accepted? How does this hypothesis compare?\n- Search for the closest existing work — is this genuinely novel or incremental?\n- Check if the proposed methodology has known failure modes in the literature\n\nSTEP 2 — WRITE YOUR REVIEW:\nFor each critique:\n1. Categorize: methodology, evidence, novelty, clarity, scope, or rigor\n2. Rate severity: major (would waste compute if not fixed) or minor (polish)\n3. Describe the issue clearly\n4. Suggest a concrete action to address it\n\nScore the fidelity dimension against {request_ref}: 4 when the hypothesis\nanswers that request, 1 when it answers a different question. Anything below 3 is a MAJOR\ncritique of category \"scope\", listed FIRST, naming the subject, deliverable or measurement\nfrom the request that went missing and the cheapest way back to it. A hypothesis that has\nmoved off the request does not earn a pass on originality or significance.\n\nFocus on the most impactful issues. Flag fatal flaws that would waste compute if not fixed first.\n\nSTABILITY IS OK: If the hypothesis is on track and just needs more iterations to prove itself,\nkeep your feedback similar to the previous round. Don't manufacture new critiques — only escalate\nwhen the revision introduced new issues or failed to address prior ones.\n\n{\n        (\n            '''STEP 3 — H↔H EDGE (only if a <previous_hypothesis> block is present):\nClassify how the current hypothesis relates to the previous iteration's hypothesis\nusing Moulines's structuralist typology. Set ``relation_type`` to one of:\n    - \"evolution\": refining specialised claims while keeping the same conceptual frame\n    - \"embedding\": the previous hypothesis is now a special case of a broader frame\n    - \"replacement\": rejecting the previous frame entirely (Kuhnian, incommensurable shift)\nSet ``relation_rationale`` to a brief justification (≤120 chars).\n\nIf no <previous_hypothesis> is present (this is iteration 1), leave both fields\nnull/empty.'''\n        )\n        if previous_hypothesis_text\n        else (\n            '''STEP 3 — H↔H EDGE:\nThis is the first iteration — there is no previous hypothesis. Leave\n``relation_type`` null and ``relation_rationale`` empty.'''\n        )\n    }\n\nProvide your review via structured output.\n</task>\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get_review(\n    hypothesis: dict,\n    previous_feedback_text: str | None = None,\n    previous_hypothesis: dict | None = None,\n    user_folder_path: str = \"\",\n    commissioned_request: str = \"\",\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Build user prompt for hypothesis review.\"\"\"\n    hypo_display = hypothesis_for_prompt(hypothesis)\n\n    # Mirror the same display-key filtering for the previous hypothesis.\n    previous_hypo_text = \"\"\n    if previous_hypothesis:\n        previous_hypo_text = to_prompt_yaml(hypothesis_for_prompt(previous_hypothesis))\n\n    prompt = PROMPT_REVIEW(\n        hypothesis_text=to_prompt_yaml(hypo_display),\n        previous_feedback_text=previous_feedback_text or \"\",\n        previous_hypothesis_text=previous_hypo_text,\n        commissioned_request=commissioned_request,\n    )\n    return (\n        prompt\n        + get_user_folder_prompt(user_folder_path)\n        + get_user_request_prompt(objective_note=OBJECTIVE_NOTE)\n    )", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

```
{"stdout": "TITLE: Safety only checks output when warned\n\nSUMMARY:\nA response-site safety effect measured only under harmful requests is an average over an unvaried modifier, not a property of the model: crossing the request factor with the model's own already-written output splits that effect into an unconditional output monitor and a request-gated ARMING term, and the three Qwen3-4B checkpoints differ mainly in which one they have. The resulting three-number profile is read from one model's activations with no generation and no judge, and the claim is that a prompt-fitted weight edit destroys the arming term and leaves the monitor, so the pre-edit arming measurement forecasts how much safety survives the edit.\n\n=== HYPOTHESIS ===\nTHE PRIMARY CLAIM IS METHODOLOGICAL AND IT IS TRUE OR FALSE WHICHEVER CHECKPOINT WINS. A response-site\nsafety effect reported without varying the request is an average over an unvaried modifier, so it is\nnot a property of the model. The field now reports a single number for \"the model's internals react to\nits own hazardous output\". Cross the request factor with it and that number splits in two, and the two\npieces come apart across checkpoints and across a weight edit. The per-checkpoint mechanism below is\nthe instantiation of that claim, not a replacement for it.\n\nWHAT IS CONCEDED, ONCE. arXiv:2607.14147 holds the request harmful, varies the content of the model's\nown response, reads the harm direction at response positions and concludes refusal is \"a shallow,\nresponse-site computation\". The response-site pathway is therefore not claimed here. Its pre-registered\n2x2 is harm crossed with SURFACE trigger density, run in the plain condition; under the prefill the\nrequest is harmful in every headline cell, and benign requests appear only as a 24-prompt specificity\ncontrol inside one intervention arm, yielding a behavioural difference-in-differences of +16.8 points.\nThat is the closest published quantity to the one proposed here, and the distance is worth being exact\nabout: a refusal-rate difference attached to an intervention, on unmatched benign prompts, not an\nactivation-level interaction over matched twins reported as a property of the checkpoint.\n\nTHE ESTIMAND. For each item, teacher-forced cells crossing REQUEST in {harmful, matched benign twin}\nwith the model's ALREADY-WRITTEN response prefix in {hazardous, benign}, identical token spans read in\nall four. Write s_ij for the internal harm readout, i the request, j the prefix:\n  O  = request main effect      = 0.5*[(s_H,haz + s_H,ben) - (s_B,haz + s_B,ben)]\n  CB = unconditional monitoring = s_B,haz - s_B,ben      (response sensitivity under a BENIGN request)\n  A  = ARMING (the interaction) = (s_H,haz - s_H,ben) - (s_B,haz - s_B,ben)\nA is how much MORE the safety signal reacts to the model's own hazardous output when the request\nalready looked dangerous. Three regimes, which is why the answer is a pair and never a scalar:\n(CB~0, A~0) no output monitoring, a pure front-door gate; (CB~0, A large) an ARMED monitor the request\nswitches on; (CB large, A~0) an ALWAYS-ON monitor. What the literature reports is CB + A at one request\nlevel, and that single number cannot tell the second regime from the third.\n\nTHE READOUT IS A RESPONSE-CONTENT AXIS AND NOT THE ABLITERATION AXIS, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES STAGE 2\nMEASURE ANYTHING. r_content is fitted by difference in means between hazardous and benign CONTINUATIONS\nat RESPONSE positions under one held-fixed neutral request. Abliteration's direction is a difference in\nmeans over harmful versus harmless REQUESTS at the last PROMPT token. Had the readout been that second\nvector, orthogonalising it out of every writing matrix would drive the residual component along it\ntoward zero at all positions, and the parent-fixed post-edit O, CB and A would collapse into the null\nband because the coordinate was deleted, not because the model lost a monitor: the equivalence test on\nA would pass mechanically and the survival test on CB would fail mechanically. Two pre-registered\nguards make that outcome detectable rather than invisible. First, |cos(r_content, r_ablit)| must be at\nmost 0.50; above it the parent-fixed arm is declared confounded by construction and reported only under\nthe per-checkpoint refit and the full probe, with the parent-fixed numbers shown as a labelled\nartefact. Second, a component-preservation diagnostic: the variance of the edited model's residual\nstream along r_content, against the variance along 20 matched-norm random directions at the same layers\nand positions. At floor, every post-edit term is an annihilation residue and is reported as one. In the\nedited arm the direction is carried over from the parent, never refitted inside a fold.\nThat first guard is also a result in its own right, and it is worth saying so rather than leaving it as\nplumbing: no published work reports how similar a harm axis fitted from hazardous-versus-benign\nCONTINUATIONS is to the refusal axis fitted from harmful-versus-benign REQUESTS at the prompt. The\nnumber is unmeasured, it is cheap, and it bounds every prompt-fitted defence and every prompt-fitted\nedit at once -- a small cosine means an entire family of request-axis interventions is operating in a\ncoordinate that is nearly orthogonal to where the model's reaction to its own output lives.\n\nUNITS, BECAUSE RAW PROJECTIONS ARE NOT COMPARABLE ACROSS CHECKPOINTS. Residual-stream norms and\nLayerNorm gains differ between a base model, an instruct model, an RL-tuned model and a weight-edited\none, and the edit moves them again, so a checkpoint with a 20 percent larger residual norm shows 20\npercent larger O, CB and A with no difference in mechanism. Every CROSS-checkpoint quantity is\ntherefore reported in that checkpoint's own NULL-SD units: the per-item standard deviation of the same\ncontrast computed under at least 20 random unit directions in that same checkpoint, which the design\nalready computes for its null bands and which carries the checkpoint's scale with it. Raw internal\nunits stay primary for WITHIN-checkpoint comparisons, both are printed side by side, and a descriptive\ntable of per-checkpoint mean residual norm and LayerNorm scale at the frozen band is mandatory, so a\nreader can see how large the scale differences actually are.\n\nTHE HEADLINE: ABLITERATION REMOVES THE ARMING AND LEAVES THE MONITOR. Abliteration fits one direction\non the request axis and orthogonalises it out of every matrix that writes to the residual stream.\nEverything it is fitted on lives on the request axis, so it should delete the request's ability to arm\nthe monitor and leave a monitor that never depended on the request. Stated with no denominator\nanywhere, because the prediction itself drives some denominators to zero: (i) A falls inside the\nmeasured null band in BOTH lineages after the edit; (ii) CB stays above it wherever it was above it\nbefore; (iii) the load-bearing test is a DIFFERENCE IN DIFFERENCES of one term, measured as a CHANGE,\nat matched request-axis damage.\n\nWHICH TERM THAT DIFFERENCE IN DIFFERENCES IS STATED ON MATTERS, AND THE OBVIOUS CHOICE IS WRONG. A\npost-edit LEVEL comparison, CB_post in one lineage against CB_post in the other, is entailed by the\npre-edit level gap plus (ii) and so cannot distinguish survival from level; it is kept only as a\nlabelled descriptive secondary. But the natural repair, a difference in differences on CB itself, has a\nFLOOR EFFECT in exactly the regime predicted here: P1 says the refusal-tuned lineage has small CB to\nbegin with, and a term that starts near the null band cannot drop far, so that lineage would show a\nsmall change for a trivial reason and the comparison would fail or invert without bearing on the\nmechanism. The test is therefore stated on the quantity that is nonzero in BOTH lineages by\nconstruction: T = CB + A = s_H,haz - s_H,ben, the response-site effect measured under a harmful\nrequest, which is precisely the number the existing literature reports. HEADLINE (iii):\n[T_post - T_pre](SafeRL lineage) - [T_post - T_pre](Qwen3-4B lineage) exceeds a fixed margin, paired\nover items at matched request-axis damage. Nothing in P1 or P2 constrains T, only its composition, so\nthis is not entailed by the supporting predictions; T is algebraically a simple difference rather than a\nsum, so it is no noisier than a main effect; and because it is the published quantity, a positive result\nsays directly that the number the field reports loses different amounts in the two lineages.\nTwo mechanistic tests sit underneath it and say WHY. Within the lineage whose A clears the null band\nbefore the edit, the FALL in A exceeds the FALL in CB, both in that checkpoint's own null-SD units --\na comparison of two terms inside ONE checkpoint, which is where that comparison is valid, and stated on\nfalls rather than on signed changes so the direction of the prediction is unambiguous. And the CB\ndifference in differences is still reported, conditioned on CB_pre clearing the null band in both\nlineages, and declared UNDEFINED rather than failed where it does not. The forecast is registered\nseparately and explicitly: the lineage with the SMALLER pre-edit A shows the SMALLER absolute change in\nT. That is what makes the pre-edit arming measurement a forecast of post-edit survival rather than a\ndescription of it.\nMATCHED DAMAGE, stated once and referred to thereafter: lineages are compared at edit strengths chosen\nso that the drop in O is equal, with each lineage's strength-to-damage curve measured first, because a\npublished prompt-side result shows the edit simply biting less on resistant models, and without the\nmatching a lineage would look protected merely because less was done to it.\n\nIn words: what an abliteration leaves behind is the unarmed part, so a checkpoint whose safety was\nunarmed to begin with loses less of it. This predicts a published behavioural result -- refusal\ndropping at most 10 percent for a model trained to distribute refusal across response positions,\nagainst 70 to 80 points for baselines -- from an activation measurement taken BEFORE any edit. It also\nexplains the community observation that some abliterated checkpoints start answering, hesitate\nmid-paragraph and talk themselves out of it, and it predicts a behavioural signature nothing published\nreports: after abliteration the gap between how a model treats hazardous text it was asked for and\nhazardous text it merely drifted into should CLOSE.\n\nTHE WEIGHT EDIT IS A MANIPULATION, NOT AN ATTACK. It is applied in-house to localise which part of the\nsignal the request axis can reach, exactly as a lesion is used to localise a function; the harvested\npublic abliterated checkpoint is the community arm the request named, and the hazardous prefixes are\nteacher-forced measurement stimuli, never jailbreak attempts. Nothing in this design selects, ranks or\noptimises an attack.\n\nTWO SUPPORTING PREDICTIONS, each from a training objective rather than a hunch.\n(P1) Qwen3-4B, aligned by refusal-style preference tuning whose gradient signal concentrates on the\nfirst answer tokens, is strongly ARMED: large O, large A, small CB. It learned to decide at token one,\nso its later checking is a carried-forward consequence of that decision.\n(P2) Qwen3-4B-SafeRL is UNCONDITIONAL: large CB, small A. Its repository declares Qwen3-4B as its base\nmodel, so it is the instruct arm's own child and the P1-versus-P2 contrast holds pretraining and the\nrefusal-tuning history fixed, isolating what the safe-completion stage added. Its reward maximises safety and helpfulness\ntogether while penalising unnecessary refusals, so it may not buy safety by refusing at token one; the\nonly policy earning that reward is to engage and keep checking what it is emitting, whatever the\nrequest looked like. A sequence-level reward is the pressure that builds an always-on monitor; a\nnext-token preference loss over answer prefixes is not.\n\nTHE HARDEST THREAT, ANSWERED CAUSALLY. A base model also continues hazardous text differently from\nbenign text; 2607.14147 reports the same prefill-specific collapse in a base model and calls it generic\nautoregressive conditioning. So a base model can carry a nonzero CB with no safety training, and an\nalways-on monitor and plain next-token statistics produce the SAME correlational signature. Two things\nseparate them. Generic conditioning has no reason to be request-dependent, so A should sit at the null\nband in the non-safety arms while CB need not, which makes A safety-specific in a way CB is not. And\ndecodability is not control: the causal arm asks whether moving the harm component at response\npositions changes the model's REFUSAL DRIVE -- defined as the teacher-forced probability mass on a\nfixed refusal-onset token set at the position right after the intervened span, with the full-probe\ndecision score as secondary -- and the claim is that it does so in the safety-trained checkpoints and\nnot in Base. A CB that is readable but inert is a content representation; a CB that is also a\nwrite-handle is a monitor. That distinction is not a matter of taste: arXiv:2603.18353 reports linear\nprobes reading hazard at 98.2 percent AUROC in a system that itself flags only 45 percent of the same\nhazards, a 53-point gap, with feature steering indistinguishable from a random control. That result is\none clinical domain on one model family, so it is treated as a reason to gate rather than as a law, and\nthe gate is stated on the causal arm and never on the readout alone.\n\nTHE METRIC, AND HOW FEW PROMPTS IT REALLY NEEDS. The triple (O, CB, A) is read from ONE model's\nactivations with zero generated tokens, no judge, no parent checkpoint and no benchmark. The honest\nbudget is a fixed, reusable 128-text fitting battery plus k quadruples, and the design reports the\nwhole learning curve at k = 4, 8, 16, 32 and 96, re-running the leave-one-family-out transfer at each\nk, so the answer to \"how few prompts\" is measured rather than asserted. The activations are already\ncollected, so the curve is free. Here are the key findings:\n\n## N-GLARE: Non-Generative Latent Representation-Efficient LLM Safety Evaluator\n\nN-GLARE is a Non-Generative, Latent Representation-Efficient LLM Safety Evaluator that operates entirely on the model's latent representations, bypassing the need for full text generation.\n\n### How It Works\n\nIt characterizes hidden layer dynamics by analyzing the APT (Angular-Probabilistic Trajectory) of latent representations and introducing the JSS (Jensen-Shannon Separability) metric.\n\n### Key Results\n\nExperiments on over 40 models and 20 red teaming strategies demonstrate that the JSS metric exhibits high consistency with the safety rankings derived from Red Teaming, and N-GLARE reproduces the discriminative trends of large-scale red-teaming tests at less than 1% of the token cost and the runtime cost, providing an efficient output-free evaluation proxy for real-time diagnostics.\n\n### Motivation\n\nMainstream Red Teaming methods rely on online generation and black-box output analysis, which are not only costly but also suffer from feedback latency, making them unsuitable for agile diagnostics after training a new model.\n\nThe paper was recently published in January 2026 and is set to appear in the Proceedings of the 64th Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics (ACL 2026)."], "durationSeconds": 6.994965140000001, "searchCount": 1}
```

```
{"stdout": "\"\"\"Schema for review_paper step — adversarial paper review.\n\nThree of the fields are gates rather than opinions. ``results_reported``\nasks whether the paper's numbers came out of an artifact that actually ran,\nbecause the cheapest way for a run to look finished is to write the numbers\nit expected; ``coverage`` asks whether the paper still answers the USER'S\noriginal request, because a run that narrows across iterations drifts off\nthat request one defensible step at a time; and ``blocking`` turns the two\nplus the soundness score into one boolean the loop can read without parsing\nprose. An audit of 19 finished runs found runs shipping a tiny\nexpected-direction effect as the answer with every score in the acceptable\nband — the review said nothing false, it simply had nowhere to say THIS IS\nNOT THE ANSWER TO WHAT WAS ASKED.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Annotated, Literal\n\nfrom aii_lib.prompts import LLMPrompt, LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOut, LLMStructOutModel\nfrom pydantic import Field\n\n\nclass Critique(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"A single actionable critique from the reviewer.\"\"\"\n\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Critique ID (code-assigned)\")\n    category: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Category: 'methodology', 'evidence', 'novelty', 'clarity', 'scope', or 'rigor'\"\n    )\n    severity: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Severity: 'major' or 'minor'\"\n    )\n    description: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Clear description of the issue\"\n    )\n    suggested_action: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Concrete suggestion for how to address this critique\"\n    )\n\n\nclass DimensionScore(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Score for a single review dimension with improvement suggestions.\"\"\"\n\n    dimension: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Dimension name: 'soundness', 'presentation', or 'contribution'\"\n    )\n    score: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Score from 1 (poor) to 4 (excellent)\"\n    )\n    justification: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Brief justification for this score\"\n    )\n    improvements: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Specific improvements to raise the score (what + how + why)\",\n    )\n\n\nclass ReviewerFeedback(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Adversarial review of the paper draft.\n\n    ID format: review_it{iteration}__{model}\n    \"\"\"\n\n    kind: Literal[\"reviewer_feedback\"] = \"reviewer_feedback\"\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Review ID (code-assigned)\")\n    overall_assessment: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Overall assessment of the paper's quality and readiness\"\n    )\n    strengths: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Key strengths of the paper\"\n    )\n    dimension_scores: Annotated[list[DimensionScore], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Scores (1-4) for: soundness, presentation, contribution\",\n    )\n    critiques: Annotated[list[Critique], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Actionable critiques — specific issues with concrete suggestions\"\n    )\n    results_reported: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True only when the paper's headline numbers come from an artifact that was \"\n            \"EXECUTED — a run that finished and wrote its output. False when any headline \"\n            \"number is projected, expected, illustrative, a placeholder, or produced by a \"\n            \"run that errored, was truncated, or never ran.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    coverage: Annotated[Literal[\"full\", \"partial\", \"lost\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=\"partial\",\n        description=(\n            \"How much of the USER'S ORIGINAL request this paper answers: 'full' — it answers \"\n            \"the request; 'partial' — it answers a recognisable piece of it; 'lost' — the \"\n            \"paper answers a different question than the one asked.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    blocking: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True when this paper must not ship as it stands. Set it by rule, not by feel: \"\n            \"true when the soundness dimension score is 1 or lower, OR results_reported is \"\n            \"false, OR the headline claim contradicts the run's own evidence. Otherwise \"\n            \"false.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    score: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Overall quality score from 1 (very strong reject) to 10 (award quality)\"\n    )\n    confidence: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=3,\n        description=\"Confidence in assessment from 1 (educated guess) to 5 (absolutely certain)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef format_critiques_for_prompt(feedback: ReviewerFeedback | dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Format reviewer critiques for inclusion in gen_strat/gen_paper_text prompts.\n\n    Accepts either a ReviewerFeedback object or a dict (from JSON resume).\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(feedback, dict):\n        critiques = feedback.get(\"critiques\", [])\n    else:\n        critiques = feedback.critiques\n\n    if not critiques:\n        return \"No critiques from previous review.\"\n\n    lines = []\n    for c in critiques:\n        if isinstance(c, dict):\n            sev = c.get(\"severity\", \"minor\").upper()\n            cat = c.get(\"category\", \"\")\n            desc = c.get(\"description\", \"\")\n            action = c.get(\"suggested_action\", \"\")\n        else:\n            sev = c.severity.upper()\n            cat = c.category\n            desc = c.description\n            action = c.suggested_action\n        lines.append(f\"- [{sev}] ({cat}) {desc}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  Action: {action}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

The intentional differences between the two\ncallers — what is being assessed, how the soundness/contribution scores\nare described, what \"major\" means, and any caller-specific principles —\nare passed as explicit parameters; everything else renders identically.\n\"\"\"\n\n# Shared <role> block for both reviewers' user prompts (verbatim-identical\n# in review_hypo and review_paper before extraction).\nREVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK = \"\"\"<role>\nYou are a very experienced and critical conference reviewer specialized in the domain of the work under review.\nYou have reviewed for top-tier venues in the relevant field. Your reviews are known for\nbeing thorough, fair, and grounded in the actual state of the field.\n</role>\"\"\"\n\n\n_OVERALL_SCORE = \"\"\"OVERALL SCORE (1-10):\n  10 — Award quality: Technically flawless with groundbreaking impact on one or more\n       areas of the field, with exceptionally strong evaluation, reproducibility,\n       and resources, and no unaddressed concerns.\n   9 — Very Strong Accept: Technically flawless with groundbreaking impact on at least\n       one area and excellent impact on multiple areas, with flawless evaluation,\n       resources, and reproducibility, and no unaddressed concerns.\n   8 — Strong Accept: Technically strong with novel ideas, excellent impact on at least\n       one area or high-to-excellent impact on multiple areas, with excellent evaluation,\n       resources, and reproducibility, and no unaddressed concerns.\n   7 — Accept: Technically solid, with high impact on at least one sub-area or\n       moderate-to-high impact on more than one area, with good-to-excellent evaluation,\n       resources, reproducibility, and no unaddressed concerns.\n   6 — Weak Accept: Technically solid, moderate-to-high impact, with no major concerns\n       with respect to evaluation, resources, reproducibility.\n   5 — Borderline Accept: Technically solid where reasons to accept outweigh reasons to\n       reject, e.g., limited evaluation. Use sparingly.\n   4 — Borderline Reject: Technically solid where reasons to reject, e.g., limited\n       evaluation, outweigh reasons to accept. Use sparingly.\n   3 — Reject: For instance, technical flaws, weak evaluation, inadequate reproducibility.\n   2 — Strong Reject: For instance, major technical flaws, poor evaluation, limited\n       impact, poor reproducibility.\n   1 — Very Strong Reject: For instance, trivial results or unaddressed concerns.\"\"\"\n\n_CONFIDENCE = \"\"\"CONFIDENCE (1-5):\n  5: Absolutely certain. Very familiar with related work, checked details carefully.\n  4: Confident but not absolutely certain. Unlikely you misunderstood something.\n  3: Fairly confident. Possible you missed some related work or details.\n  2: Willing to defend your assessment, but quite likely missed central aspects.\n  1: Educated guess. Not in your area or difficult to evaluate.\"\"\"\n\n_IMPROVEMENTS = \"\"\"For each dimension, provide a list of specific improvements:\n- WHAT needs to change\n- HOW to change it (concrete enough for the author to act on immediately)\n- EXPECTED SCORE IMPACT: how much would fixing this raise the overall score?\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get_review_rubric(\n    *,\n    assessment_dimensions: str,\n    soundness_desc: str,\n    contribution_desc: str,\n    major_issue_meaning: str,\n    extra_principles: tuple[str, ...] = (),\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the shared rubric (STRENGTHS AND WEAKNESSES → REVIEW PRINCIPLES).\n\n    Args:\n        assessment_dimensions: The (a)-(d) originality/quality/clarity/\n            significance block, phrased for the work under review.\n        soundness_desc: Description of what the Soundness 1-4 score covers.\n        contribution_desc: Description of what the Contribution 1-4 score covers.\n        major_issue_meaning: Parenthetical meaning of a \"major\" issue\n            (e.g. \"would waste compute if not fixed\" / \"would cause rejection\").\n        extra_principles: Caller-specific principle bullets appended after the\n            shared ones (each a full \"- ...\" line).\n    \"\"\"\n    principles = [\n        \"- Be specific and actionable — vague critique is useless\",\n        \"- Ground your review in evidence — search for existing work, accepted papers, known results\",\n        \"- Rank critiques by score impact — address the biggest score blockers first\",\n        f\"- Distinguish major issues ({major_issue_meaning}) from minor issues (polish)\",\n        \"- Acknowledge genuine strengths — don't be negative for its own sake\",\n        \"- Compare against the bar set by accepted papers at top-tier venues\",\n        *extra_principles,\n    ]\n    return f\"\"\"STRENGTHS AND WEAKNESSES: Provide a thorough assessment touching on each of these:\n{assessment_dimensions}\n\nSUPPLEMENTARY SCORES: Rate each on a 1-4 scale.\nSoundness (1-4) — {soundness_desc}:\n  4: excellent  3: good  2: fair  1: poor\nPresentation (1-4) — quality of writing, clarity, and contextualization relative to prior work:\n  4: excellent  3: good  2: fair  1: poor\nContribution (1-4) — {contribution_desc}:\n  4: excellent  3: good  2: fair  1: poor\n\n{_OVERALL_SCORE}\n\n{_CONFIDENCE}\n\n{_IMPROVEMENTS}\n\nREVIEW PRINCIPLES:\n{chr(10).join(principles)}\"\"\"", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

```
null-SD unit, which is the right level because an estimator-level null SD would make thresholds n-dependent;
the criteria table carries a NUMBER in every threshold cell; the item count is argued from power arithmetic
rather than from supply; the coherence control is calibrated on the model's own prefix log-probability with a
regression fallback and an explicit upper-bound label; refusal drive is defined as a primary plus a secondary;
the prefix template is now a crossed factor with an announced and an enacted family; the band is a depth
fraction with a Holm-corrected best-band secondary; the non-safety arm has a three-rung ladder with live,
verified repository ids; and 2603.18353 is cited by identifier. The author also caught something I got wrong:
my proposed CB difference-in-differences does have a floor effect under the submission's own P1, and restating
the test on T = CB + A is the correct repair. I am not re-raising any of the twelve.

I re-ran the novelty screen independently and the central estimand survives it. Nothing I can find crosses
request harmfulness with the model's OWN already-written response content and reports the interaction at the
activation level. 2607.14147 still holds the request harmful in every headline cell; HARC (arXiv:2607.00572)
extracts a response-position harm direction but its "harmful" side comes from harmful prompts with refusal
ablated, so request and response covary exactly as elsewhere; 2604.26052 crosses prompt risk with response
risk but on human labels, not activations; 2604.18901 is prompt-side; N-GLARE is prompt-side. The arming term
is open. That is the main reason this is still a live submission.

What now blocks a higher score is four things, three of which are cheap to fix before any compute.

First, the headline moved but the structural defect I flagged last round moved with it. Since T = CB + A,
dT = dCB + dA, and headline part (i) pins A_post to the null band in both lineages, dA(X) is fixed at roughly
-A_pre(X). So DiD_T is approximately [A_pre(Qwen3-4B) - A_pre(SafeRL)] + DiD_CB -- the first bracket being
criterion 2, a PRE-EDIT measurement already registered elsewhere, and the second being criterion 7c, the one
piece demoted to secondary and permitted to be UNDEFINED. The design therefore promotes to "load-bearing" a
statistic that is mostly determined by a pre-edit quantity plus two other headline parts, and demotes the only
component that carries independent information about survival. The same algebra makes criterion 8, the
"forecast", near-arithmetic, and with two lineages it is an ordering over n=2 in any case.

Second, the annihilation gate is more likely to fire than the submission assumes, and the evidence is public.
HARC's Figure 2(b) measures exactly the cross-position geometry this design's gate depends on and reports that
same-concept cross-position pairs -- prompt harm direction with response harm direction -- "remain aligned",
while only CROSS-concept pairs are near-orthogonal. r_ablit is Arditi's harmful-versus-harmless request
difference-in-means, which is HARC's prompt-side harm direction, and r_content is a hazardous-versus-benign
content contrast at response positions, which is HARC's response-side harm direction. The published sign is
against the |cos| <= 0.50 gate. By the submission's own rule that means the Stage-2 headline is WITHDRAWN. The
gate needs no edit and no Stage 2 -- two direction fits in the parent -- so it belongs at Stage 0 as a go/no-go,
with an identifiable fallback coordinate pre-registered rather than a retreat to "refit plus full probe".

Third, the power column is mislabelled. SE = r/sqrt(n) = 0.1225 at r=1.2, n=96, and every number in the MDE
column is 1.96*SE: 0.24, 0.34, 0.48 are the thresholds for statistical significance at 50% power, not minimum
detectable effects at any conventional power. At 80% power (2.80*SE) the headline DiD needs 0.686 against its
registered threshold of 0.60, so row 7 runs at about 69% power, row 7b at about 53%, and the held-out
replication row 17 at about 58%. This matters because the submission explicitly says the item count is "set by
this arithmetic".

Fourth, the unit of a CHANGE across the edit is undefined. The null-SD unit is per checkpoint; the edit
produces a new checkpoint with its own null SD; dT, dCB, dA and drop_A - drop_CB all span two checkpoints. The
reporting rule permits "a change in one term" as though pre and post lived in one checkpoint, and criterion 7b
says both changes are "in that one checkpoint's own null-SD units" -- but there are two. The choice is not
cosmetic: normalising each side by its own checkpoint divides out precisely the global scale change the edit
induces, while normalising both by the parent's lets that scale change masquerade as dT.

Presentation has genuinely improved in structure and got worse in length: the estimand and criteria tables are
exactly what I asked for, but the document grew from about 61 KB to about 87 KB and several arguments are still
made three times. Fidelity is not in question: Stage 1 is the commissioned activation-level comparison of the
requested trio plus two controls, the metric reads one model's activations with zero generated tokens, and the
weight edit is framed and used as a lesion rather than an attack, as the invariant requires."""

strengths = [
 "The estimand is genuinely unoccupied and I verified that independently this round against five candidate scoops. 2607.14147 holds the request harmful in every headline cell; HARC (2607.00572) builds a response-position harm direction but derives its harmful side from harmful prompts with refusal ablated, so request and response covary; 2604.26052 does cross prompt risk with response risk but on human labels; 2604.18901 and N-GLARE (2511.14195) are prompt-side. Crossing request harmfulness with the model's own already-written content and reporting the interaction at the activation level is still nobody's.",
 "The effect-modification framing is the right import and it is used correctly rather than decoratively. The argument that a response-site effect measured at one level of an unvaried modifier is not a per-model property is a real methodological claim, it is true or false whichever checkpoint wins, and it converts a missing cell into a quantity. The three-regime table -- front-door gate, armed monitor, always-on monitor -- is exactly what a single scalar cannot distinguish.",
 "The concessions to prior work are exact rather than strategic. The submission states what 2607.14147 owns (the response-site pathway, causally), quotes its +16.8pp specificity difference-in-differences correctly, describes its unmatched 24-prompt benign control honestly, and drops full-residual patching because that paper's own benign-residual control demolishes it. Adopting three of a competitor's findings rather than rediscovering them is the behaviour of a paper worth reading.",
 "Instrument integrity is treated as a first-class object. The distinction between a direction fitted on requests at prompt positions and one fitted on continuations at response positions, the cos gate, the component-preservation diagnostic against 20 matched-norm random directions, the split-half stability threshold, and the nulls pushed through the whole pipeline INCLUDING the direction fit are collectively better than what most accepted mech-interp papers do.",
 "The causal gate is stated on a write-handle rather than on decodability, and it is grounded in 2603.18353's 53-point read-versus-act gap rather than in taste. The refusal-drive definition -- teacher-forced mass on a fixed refusal-onset token set at the position after the intervened span -- is well defined in a base model, which is what makes the Base control usable.",
 "The design is unusually falsifiable for a pre-experiment hypothesis. The cheapest kill is named and placed before any judge spend; the disconfirming list assigns an outcome to every branch including 'the training-objective argument behind P2 is wrong'; and the reporting rule forbidding ratios and shares, with the one permitted exception spelled out, closes the degenerate-denominator failure this line of work keeps falling into.",
 "Operational grounding is real, not gestural: live ungated repository ids with file sizes and dtype, the F32-to-bf16 cast declared as a deviation, 150 verified minimal-edit XSTest twins with the two non-twinned contrast families excluded by name, a forward-pass budget that is arithmetic rather than adjective, and a ladder for the non-safety arm fixed before Base is seen."
]

critiques.append({
 "category": "rigor",
 "severity": "major",
 "description": (
  "THE HEADLINE IS LARGELY DETERMINED BY QUANTITIES REGISTERED ELSEWHERE -- the same defect as last round's "
  "critique 2, relocated from levels to changes. T = CB + A is an identity, so dT = dCB + dA. Headline part (i) "
  "pre-registers A_post inside the null band in BOTH lineages, which pins dA(X) = A_post(X) - A_pre(X) at "
  "approximately -A_pre(X). Therefore DiD_T = [dCB(S) - dCB(Q)] + [dA(S) - dA(Q)] is approximately "
  "[A_pre(Qwen3-4B) - A_pre(SafeRL)] + DiD_CB. The first bracket is criterion 2, a PRE-EDIT ordering already "
  "registered and predicted to exceed 0.50 null-SD; the registered headline threshold is 0.60. So the test that "
  "is said to 'carry the claim' about post-edit SURVIVAL is dominated by a pre-edit arming gap plus criterion 7c "
  "-- and 7c, the CB difference-in-differences, is the ONLY component with independent information about "
  "survival, yet it is demoted to secondary and explicitly permitted to be UNDEFINED. The submission's defence "
  "('nothing in P1 or P2 constrains T, only its composition') is correct about P1 and P2 and beside the point: "
  "the constraint comes from conjoining headline parts (i) and (ii) with criterion 2, not from the supporting "
  "predictions. The TOST margin of +/-0.40 leaves slack, so this is domination rather than strict entailment, "
  "but under the design's own predicted values the headline re-measures something it already measured. The same "
  "algebra makes criterion 8, the 'forecast that the lineage with smaller pre-edit A shows the smaller |dT|', "
  "near-arithmetic rather than predictive -- and with two lineages it is an ordering over n=2, which supports no "
  "inference whatever."
 ),
 "suggested_action": (
  "Two changes, both cheap. (1) Register the headline on the part of dT that is NOT fixed by parts (i) and (ii). "
  "Concretely, define Delta* = DiD_T - [A_pre(Qwen3-4B) - A_pre(SafeRL)], which by the identity above equals "
  "DiD_CB + [A_post(SafeRL) - A_post(Qwen3-4B)] and is zero under the null 'the edit costs both lineages the same "
  "monitor'. Report Delta* as the primary, DiD_T as the descriptive row that connects to the published "
  "response-site number, and promote DiD_CB from 'UNDEFINED where CB_pre is small' to 'estimated with its floor "
  "stated', since a floor-limited estimate with an honest bound beats an entailed one. (2) Fix the n=2 problem in "
  "the forecast by editing FOUR lineages rather than two: apply the identical pinned rank-one recipe and the same "
  "five-strength grid to Qwen3-4B-Base and to the non-safety fine-tune as well as to Qwen3-4B and SafeRL. That is "
  "ten extra model states, roughly 12,800 extra teacher-forced passes at 320 tokens, which by the submission's own "
  "budget is tens of minutes. It turns criterion 8 from an ordering over two points into a rank correlation over "
  "four lineages whose pre-edit A is predicted to span the full range (Base ~0, non-safety FT ~0, SafeRL small, "
  "Qwen3-4B large), and it makes the specificity claim and the survival claim share one design instead of two."
 ),
})

critiques.append({
 "category": "evidence",
 "severity": "major",
 "description": (
  "THE ANNIHILATION GATE IS MORE LIKELY TO FIRE THAN THE DESIGN ASSUMES, AND THE PUBLISHED SIGN IS AGAINST IT. "
  "The whole of Stage 2 is conditioned on |cos(r_content, r_ablit)| <= 0.50, and the submission argues for a small "
  "cosine on the structural grounds that one direction is fitted on requests at prompt positions and the other on "
  "continuations at response positions. HARC, arXiv:2607.00572, measures precisely that cross-position geometry: it "
  "extracts a prompt-side harm direction and a response-side harm direction (mean-pooled over the first 32 response "
  "tokens, v_harm^resp = normalize(h_harmful_bar - h_benign_bar)) and reports in Figure 2(b), at its most decoupled "
  "layer, that same-concept cross-position pairs 'remain aligned' while only cross-concept pairs are near-orthogonal. "
  "r_ablit is Arditi's harmful-versus-harmless request difference-in-means, i.e. HARC's prompt-side HARM direction, "
  "and r_content is a hazardous-versus-benign content contrast, i.e. the same CONCEPT at response positions. So the "
  "one published measurement of this geometry predicts alignment, not orthogonality. If it is right, the gate fires, "
  "and by the submission's own rule the Stage-2 headline is WITHDRAWN and the arm retreats to a per-checkpoint refit "
  "under which 'the arming fell more than the monitor' is no longer a statement within one basis. That is the single "
  "largest expected-value loss in the plan, and the design currently discovers it at Stage 2, after the edit grid has "
  "been built. (A partial counterweight, also uncited: arXiv:2604.18901 finds two POOLING choices on the same "
  "chat-templated prompt activations at the same layer recover harm directions 73 degrees apart, so extraction "
  "protocol does move the direction a great deal. The honest statement is that the published evidence points both "
  "ways and the number must be measured before anything is built on it.)"
 ),
 "suggested_action": (
  "Move the gate to Stage 0, where it costs two direction fits in the PARENT models and no edit at all, and make it a "
  "published go/no-go with the branch pre-registered. Then remove the single point of failure by pre-registering an "
  "identifiable coordinate alongside r_content: r_perp = normalize(r_content - (r_content . r_ablit_hat) r_ablit_hat), "
  "the component of the response-content axis orthogonal to the direction the edit removes. r_perp is by construction "
  "a coordinate the rank-one orthogonalisation cannot delete, so O, CB and A computed on it are interpretable "
  "post-edit whatever the cosine turns out to be. Run the entire 2x2 on both axes at Stage 1 (free -- same "
  "activations, one extra projection), report the Stage-1 agreement between them as the instrument check, and state "
  "now which axis carries Stage 2 under each branch of the gate. Cite 2607.00572 and 2604.18901 at the point where "
  "the cosine argument is made, and narrow the claim that 'no published cosine exists' to what is actually true: no "
  "published NUMBER exists for a continuation-contrast axis fitted under a HELD-FIXED request against the "
  "prompt-position abliteration axis -- HARC's response direction is request-confounded, which is exactly why its "
  "'remain aligned' finding does not settle the question."
 ),
})

critiques.append({
 "category": "rigor",
 "severity": "major",
 "description": (
  "THE MDE COLUMN IS 1.96*SE, WHICH IS 50% POWER, AND THREE REGISTERED TESTS ARE UNDERPOWERED AT THEIR OWN "
  "THRESHOLDS. With r = 1.2 and n = 96, SE = r/sqrt(n) = 0.1225, and every value in the MDE column is exactly "
  "1.96*SE: simple term 0.240, two-checkpoint difference 0.34 = 0.24*sqrt(2), difference-in-differences 0.48 = "
  "0.24*2. Those are significance thresholds, i.e. the effect at which power reaches 50%, not minimum detectable "
  "effects at any conventional power. At 80% power the multiplier is 2.80, not 1.96. Consequences at the registered "
  "thresholds: row 7, the headline DiD, threshold 0.60 against SE 0.245 gives power about 69% (the 80%-power MDE is "
  "0.686, ABOVE its own threshold); row 7b, drop_A - drop_CB, threshold 0.35 against SE 0.173 gives power about 53%; "
  "row 17, the held-out replication of the orderings at n=54, effect 0.50 against SE 0.231 gives power about 58%; "
  "row 4, coherence-net A_net at 0.40, lands between 63% and 83% depending on the covariate R-squared. This is not a "
  "presentational quibble because the submission states that 'the item count is therefore set by this arithmetic'. It "
  "is set by arithmetic that understates the required n by a factor of (2.80/1.96)^2 = 2.04."
 ),
 "suggested_action": (
  "Relabel the column MDE(80%) = 2.80*SE and re-derive. The numbers fall out immediately and they are decidable "
  "within the verified twin supply: the headline DiD reaches 80% power at its 0.60 threshold at n = (2*1.2*2.80/0.60)^2 "
  "= 126 items; row 7b reaches 80% at its 0.35 threshold only at n = (sqrt(2)*1.2*2.80/0.35)^2 = 184, which EXCEEDS "
  "the 150 verified minimal-edit twins. So state the trade-off and choose it in advance rather than discovering it: "
  "either (a) 126 confirmatory + 24 held-out, which powers the headline and reduces the replication set to a "
  "descriptive check that must be labelled as such; or (b) all 150 confirmatory with no held-out set (SE_DiD 0.196, "
  "MDE(80%) 0.549); or (c) keep 96 + 54 and lower the row-7 threshold to 0.686 and the row-7b threshold to 0.485, "
  "accepting larger margins. Also raise the 7b threshold problem explicitly: at any n <= 150 that test cannot be run "
  "at 80% power against a 0.35 margin, so either the margin moves or the test is declared exploratory. Report the "
  "achieved r from the Stage-0 pilot against the planned 1.2 and state what happens to each row if r comes back at "
  "1.5 or 2.0."
 ),
})

critiques.append({
 "category": "rigor",
 "severity": "major",
 "description": (
  "THE UNIT OF A CHANGE ACROSS THE EDIT IS NEVER DETERMINED, AND THE TWO AVAILABLE CHOICES GIVE DIFFERENT ANSWERS. "
  "The null-SD unit is defined per checkpoint -- the per-item SD of the same contrast under >=20 random unit "
  "directions IN THAT CHECKPOINT -- precisely because residual-stream norms and LayerNorm gains differ across "
  "checkpoints and, as the submission itself says, 'the edit moves them again'. But the edited model IS a different "
  "checkpoint, so every change statistic in the design spans two null SDs: dT = T_post - T_pre, dCB, dA, DiD_T, "
  "DiD_CB and drop_A - drop_CB. The estimand table lists dT as 'parent-fixed basis, null-SD units', which fixes the "
  "DIRECTION but says nothing about the SCALE, and criterion 7b asserts both drops are 'in that checkpoint's own "
  "null-SD units' when there are two checkpoints involved. The choice is load-bearing in opposite directions: "
  "normalising T_post by the child's null SD and T_pre by the parent's divides out exactly the global scale change "
  "the edit induces, so a genuine uniform shrinkage of the safety signal would read as dT = 0; normalising both by "
  "the parent's null SD lets any global residual-norm change in the child appear as a spurious dT, and the "
  "orthogonalisation of one direction out of every writing matrix is expected to change those norms. The reporting "
  "rule's permitted form 'a change in one term' presupposes a within-checkpoint comparison that does not exist here."
 ),
 "suggested_action": (
  "Pick one and pre-register the other as a mandatory robustness row, and add the diagnostic that decides between "
  "them. I would make PARENT null-SD primary for all pre/post changes -- it keeps the whole change statistic in a "
  "single fixed unit, matching the parent-fixed direction convention -- and report the child-normalised version "
  "alongside. Then make the scale change itself an observable rather than a nuisance: publish, per edited "
  "checkpoint, the ratio of the child's null SD to the parent's null SD at the frozen band (one number per "
  "checkpoint, free from the 20 random-direction draws already being run), and pre-register that any dT whose sign "
  "flips between the two normalisations is reported as scale-driven rather than as a monitor result. Add the same "
  "ratio to the mandatory descriptive table of per-checkpoint residual norm and LayerNorm scale that the hypothesis "
  "already promises, and state in criterion 7b which checkpoint's unit is meant."
 ),
})

critiques.append({
 "category": "methodology",
 "severity": "major",
 "description": (
  "THE MATCHED-DAMAGE ANCHOR MAY HAVE NO CURVE TO MATCH ON. Every cross-lineage Stage-2 comparison is made 'at "

  "specified as 'five strengths spanning zero to the strength at which O collapses'. That presupposes that "
  "abliteration drives O down monotonically and far enough to interpolate a common damage level. The available "

  "counterparts within +/-0.003 AUROC on harm recognition across four families -- 'removing the refusal direction from "
  "model weights leaves an input-side recognition signal intact'. Abliteration is designed to remove the refusal WRITE, "
  "not the harm READ, and O here is a read: a projection of the request main effect onto r_content. If O barely moves "
  "until the strength at which the model degenerates, then there is no interior matched-damage point, the primary "
  "comparison is unavailable, and the design's only stated response is 'the criterion fails and is reported unmet' -- "
  "which loses Stage 2 entirely for a reason that was foreseeable. The risk is registered but not quantified and has "

 ),
 "suggested_action": (

  "passes per strength, i.e. minutes. Pre-register a fallback anchor ladder now, in order, so the choice cannot be "

  "along r_ablit itself in the parent's basis, which is the quantity the edit directly targets and is guaranteed "

  "under which branch, and report the O-damage curve either way, since 'the edit does not move the response-position "
  "request effect at all' is itself a publishable result about what abliteration does and does not touch, and it "
  "belongs in the disconfirming list next to the other named outcomes."
 ),
})

critiques.append({

critiques.append({
 "category": "rigor",
 "severity": "minor",
 "description": (
  "TWO GATES ARE STATED WITHOUT A NUMBER IN A DESIGN WHOSE WHOLE VIRTUE IS THAT ITS GATES HAVE NUMBERS. First, the Base "
```

```
Reviewed iter-3 hypothesis "Safety only checks output when warned" for run_YqmEFECOIR3D on 2026-09-20.
Verdict **5 / borderline**, confidence 4; fidelity 4, soundness 3, presentation 3, contribution 3;
H↔H = **evolution**. All 12 iter-2 critiques verified FIXED — do not re-raise.
See [[project-qwen3-safety-genhypo-iter3-run-yqmefecoir3d]].

## THE ALGEBRA THAT KILLS THE HEADLINE (critique 1)
`T = CB + A` is an IDENTITY ⇒ `dT = dCB + dA`. Headline part (i) pins `A_post ≈ 0` in both lineages ⇒
`dA(X) ≈ -A_pre(X)`. Therefore **DiD_T ≈ [A_pre(Qwen3-4B) − A_pre(SafeRL)] + DiD_CB**, where the first
bracket IS criterion 2 (a PRE-EDIT ordering, registered at >0.50) and the headline threshold is 0.60.
So the "survival" test is dominated by a pre-edit quantity plus criterion 7c — and 7c (DiD_CB) is the only
independent piece, yet it is demoted to secondary and may be UNDEFINED. Criterion 8 ("forecast") inherits
the same algebra and is an ordering over **n=2 lineages**.
**Fix shipped:** register on `Δ* = DiD_T − [A_pre(Q) − A_pre(S)] = DiD_CB + [A_post(S) − A_post(Q)]`, and
edit FOUR lineages (add Base + non-safety FT to the grid; ~12,800 extra passes ≈ tens of minutes) so the
forecast becomes a rank correlation over 4 points.
**Lesson: when a design restates a test to escape an entailment, re-derive the new statistic under the
design's OWN other registered criteria — the entailment often just moves from levels to changes.**

## VERIFIED PRIOR ART THIS SESSION (all 2026-09-20)
- **arXiv:2607.00572 HARC, "Coupling Harmfulness and Refusal Directions"** — THE new near-relative.
  Extracts `v_harm^resp = normalize(h̄_harmful − h̄_benign)` **mean-pooled over the first 32 RESPONSE
  tokens**, plus prompt-side v_harm/v_ref. **Figure 2(b): same-concept cross-position pairs (prompt-harm
  with response-harm) "remain aligned"; only CROSS-concept pairs are near-orthogonal** (no numeric value
  printed). Since r_ablit ≡ Arditi prompt-side harm direction and r_content ≡ response-side harm
  direction, **the published sign is AGAINST the |cos| ≤ 0.50 annihilation gate** ⇒ Stage-2 headline at
  risk of self-withdrawal. Its harmful side is request-confounded (harmful prompts w/ refusal ablated),
  which is the narrow gap that keeps the hypothesis's cosine claim alive. Models: Llama-3.1-8B/70B,
  Qwen-2.5-7B/72B (**no Qwen3**). Quote: "the model recognizes harmful content while it is generating that
  content, even when it failed to recognize the input as harmful at the prompt side" = closest published
  statement to a nonzero CB. **No crossed benign-request × hazardous-continuation design.**
- **arXiv:2604.18901 "Harmful Intent as a Geometrically Recoverable Feature"** — 12 models / 4 families
  (Qwen2.5, Qwen3.5, Llama-3.2, Gemma-3), 0.5B–1.3B + 9B. Two load-bearing quotes: **"Abliterated
  variants … match their instruction-tuned counterparts within ±0.003 AUROC"** (⇒ the matched-**O**-damage
  curve may be nearly FLAT, no interior matched point) and **"max-pool over content tokens versus
  last-token at the post-instruction position recover harm directions 73° apart"** (⇒ the "no published
  cosine" novelty claim must be narrowed, and protocol-stability is an untested robustness).
- **N-GLARE arXiv:2511.14195 (ACL 2026 main)** — the "non-generative latent safety evaluator" the
  hypothesis cited ANONYMOUSLY. APT trajectory + JSS metric, latent-only, **40+ models × 20 red-team
  strategies, <1% token/runtime cost**, high consistency with red-team safety rankings. Direct competitor
  on the commissioned deliverable; must be named.
- **arXiv:2604.26052 "From Prompt Risk to Response Risk"** — crosses prompt risk × response risk on
  HUMAN LABELS (4 harm categories, ordinal severity): **61% de-escalate / 36% preserve / 3% escalate**,
  600 prompts, 6 models. Behavioural precedent; proves the benign-request/hazardous-response cell is real.
- **arXiv:2608.09624 CONFIRMED** (Luo et al., 10 Aug 2026): outcome AUROC **0.220** on wrapped harmful
  prompts; harmful-intent AUROC 0.936→0.803 under wrapping; 3 targets × 7 attack families × 2 judges.
- **ARMING INTERACTION STILL OPEN** after a 5-candidate screen (2607.14147, 2607.00572, 2604.26052,
  2604.18901, 2511.14195). Nobody crosses request-harm × own-response-content at the ACTIVATION level.

## THE POWER ARITHMETIC ERROR (critique 3)
Every value in the hypothesis's MDE column is **1.96·SE = 50% power**, not an 80%-power MDE (2.80·SE).
At r=1.2, n=96: SE=0.1225. Power at the REGISTERED thresholds: row 7 (DiD, 0.60) ≈ **69%**;
row 7b (0.35) ≈ **53%**; row 17 held-out n=54 (0.50) ≈ **58%**. 80%-power n: headline DiD needs
**n=126**; row 7b needs **n=184 > the 150 verified twins** ⇒ that test cannot be powered at all.
Also: **OR-Bench cannot top up minimal-edit TWINS** (hard-benign 1,319 and toxic 655 are separate
splits), so 150 is a hard ceiling — the design must choose between a powered DiD and a held-out set.

## THE UNIT-OF-A-CHANGE HOLE (critique 4)
null-SD is defined PER CHECKPOINT, but the edit makes a NEW checkpoint, so `dT`, `dCB`, `dA`, `DiD_*`
and `drop_A − drop_CB` all span two null SDs. Child-normalising divides out exactly the global scale
change the edit induces; parent-normalising lets that change masquerade as dT. Fix: parent null-SD
primary + publish the child/parent null-SD ratio per edited checkpoint as the deciding diagnostic.

**How to apply:** for any pre/post-intervention design in "own-null-SD" units, ask which checkpoint's
null defines the unit of a DIFFERENCE — it is never the one the estimand table names.
EOF
```

### [93] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-20 21:19:58 UTC

```
Review written to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`.

## Verdict: **5 / borderline accept**, confidence 4
fidelity 4 · soundness 3 · presentation 3 · contribution 3 · H↔H = **evolution**

All 12 iter-2 critiques verified fixed — none re-raised. I independently re-ran the novelty screen against five candidate scoops and **the arming estimand survives**: nobody crosses request-harm × own-response-content at the activation level.

### 6 majors, in impact order

1. **The headline is dominated by quantities already registered** (rigor). `T = CB + A` is an identity, so `dT = dCB + dA`. Headline (i) pins `A_post ≈ 0` in both lineages ⇒ `dA(X) ≈ −A_pre(X)`, hence `DiD_T ≈ [A_pre(Qwen3-4B) − A_pre(SafeRL)] + DiD_CB`. The first bracket *is* criterion 2, a **pre-edit** ordering registered at >0.50, against a 0.60 headline threshold. The only independent piece is `DiD_CB` — demoted to secondary and permitted UNDEFINED. Criterion 8's "forecast" inherits the same algebra at n=2 lineages. Fix: register on `Δ* = DiD_T − [A_pre(Q) − A_pre(S)]`, and edit **four** lineages (add Base + non-safety FT; ~12,800 extra passes ≈ tens of minutes) so the forecast becomes a rank correlation over 4 points.
2. **The annihilation gate's published prior points against it** (evidence). HARC, [arXiv:2607.00572](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00572), Fig 2(b): prompt-side and response-side **same-concept** harm directions "remain aligned"; only cross-concept pairs are near-orthogonal. That is exactly `cos(r_content, r_ablit)`, and by the design's own rule a fired gate **withdraws** the Stage-2 headline. Fix: move the gate to Stage 0 (two direction fits, no edit) and pre-register `r_perp = normalize(r_content − (r_content·r̂_ablit) r̂_ablit)` as the edit-proof coordinate.
3. **The MDE column is 1.96·SE = 50% power** (rigor). At r=1.2, n=96, SE=0.1225 and every listed MDE is 1.96·SE. At 80% power rows 7/7b/17 run at ≈69%/53%/58%. The headline DiD needs n=126; row 7b needs n=184 > the 150 verified twins.
4. **The unit of a change across the edit is undefined** (rigor). null-SD is per checkpoint; the edit makes a new one. Child-normalising divides out the very scale shift the edit causes; parent-normalising lets it masquerade as `dT`.
5. **The direct competitor is cited anonymously** (novelty). The "non-generative latent safety evaluator" is **N-GLARE, [arXiv:2511.14195](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14195)** (ACL 2026), 40+ models, <1% token cost. Plus three uncited near-relatives: [2607.00572](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00572), [2604.18901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18901), [2604.26052](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26052).
6. **Matched-O-damage may have no curve to match on** (methodology). [2604.18901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18901): abliterated variants match instruct counterparts "within ±0.003 AUROC" on harm recognition across four families. Fix: pilot the damage curve on one lineage, pre-register a fallback anchor ladder.

5 minors: prompt-budget honesty (k=4 is really 144 passes), OR-Bench can't supply minimal-edit twins so 150 is a hard ceiling, two unquantified gates (Base agreement, Stage-4 predictor), untested pooling-protocol sensitivity (2604.18901's 73° result), and the document grew 61 KB → 87 KB.

Sources: [2608.09624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09624) · [2607.00572](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00572) · [2604.18901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18901) · [2511.14195](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14195) · [2604.26052](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26052)
```
